Deferred as out-of-domain for the crypto phase.\n\n- **execSync without timeout (major):** the `git rev-parse` / `git status --porcelain` calls in the statusline hot path have no `timeout`, so a hung git invocation can freeze the status line indefinitely. Add `timeout: 1000` (+ graceful fallback) to each `execSync`.\n- **POSIX-only path handling (minor):** `cwd.split('/')` for the directory basename and the `.git/worktrees` substring check are not Windows-safe. Use `path.basename(cwd)` and normalize `gitDir` (`replace(/\\\\/g, '/')`) before the worktree-marker check.\n\n## Fix\n\nApply CodeRabbit's suggested diffs: bounded `execSync` timeouts with try/catch fallback, and `path`-helper-based basename + normalized gitDir. Keep the change scoped to `statusline.cjs`.\n\n## Source\n\nPhase 61 ship-loop CodeRabbit review, 2026-06-28 (findings on `.claude/hooks/statusline.cjs`). Non-blocking, out of the AAD-seal phase's domain.\n"

THREAD id: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6MxRrO | resolved: False | packages/crypto/src/__tests__/build-node-aad.test.ts : 352
  [greptile-apps] <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> The Rust cross-language test asserts `seal_vectors.len() == 1` (exact count), while the TS guard uses `>= 1`. If the vector file is later expanded to add a second seal vector the TS test will silently accept the changed count without any indication, while the Rust test would fail loudly. Aligning both sides to an exact count check keeps the coverage contract symmetric.

```suggestion
    // Guard: exactly 1 committed seal vector — mirrors the Rust `assert_eq!(seal_vectors.len(), 1)`.
    // If a new vector is added the count must be updated intentionally on both sides.
    expect(sealVectors.length).toBe(1);
```

Note: If this suggestion doesn't match your team's coding style, reply to this and let me know. I'll remember it for next time!
---

The ciphertext\n        // assertion above only pins the AEAD output; this asserts the high-level seal envelope\n        // is IV-FIRST and identical to the TS side: a seal/unseal pair that silently switched to\n        // `ct||iv` would pass round-trip + ciphertext checks but fail to open this IV-first blob.\n        let mut sealed_envelope = iv_arr.to_vec();\n        sealed_envelope.extend_from_slice(&ciphertext);\n        let opened = cipherbox_crypto::unseal_aes_gcm_aad(&sealed_envelope, &key_arr, &aad)\n            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!(\"unseal_aes_gcm_aad failed for {}: {:?}\", v.description, e));\n        assert_eq!(\n            hex::encode(&opened),\n            v.plaintext,\n            \"Sealed-envelope [IV][ct+tag] order mismatch for: {}\",\n            v.description\n        );\n    }"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-28-zeroize-local-key-plaintext-copies-in-aes-helpers.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-28\ntitle: Zeroize function-local key/plaintext copies in the AES-GCM helpers (project-wide)\narea: crypto\nfiles:\n  - packages/crypto/src/aes/encrypt.ts\n  - packages/crypto/src/aes/decrypt.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nCodeRabbit (Phase 61 PR #576) flagged that `encryptAesGcmAad` (`packages/crypto/src/aes/encrypt.ts`) creates function-local copies of the key and plaintext (`new Uint8Array(key)` / `new Uint8Array(plaintext)`) and leaves them for GC rather than wiping them in a `finally`, against the project guideline \"Clear sensitive data from memory after use.\"\n\nThis is **not specific to Phase 61** — the pre-existing non-AAD `encryptAesGcm` (same file, lines ~40-42) uses the identical copy pattern with no wipe, as do the decrypt helpers. The new AAD variants simply match the established pattern. Wiping only the new variant would make the codebase inconsistent.\n\nNote: JS zeroization is best-effort (no guaranteed memory control; Web Crypto copies key bytes into the internal `CryptoKey` regardless), and the callee must only wipe copies it OWNS — never the caller's reused buffers (see `[[project-zeroization-callee-must-not-zero-reused-buffer]]`). So the right fix is a single consistent pass.\n\n## Fix\n\nApply a uniform pattern across all AES-GCM helpers (`encryptAesGcm`, `encryptAesGcmAad`, and the decrypt counterparts): hold references to the function-local key/plaintext copies and `.fill(0)` them in a `finally` block after the Web Crypto call resolves. Do NOT touch caller-owned buffers. Add/adjust tests only if they assert buffer state.\n\n## Source\n\nPhase 61 ship-loop CodeRabbit review (PR #576), 2026-06-28 — `packages/crypto/src/aes/encrypt.ts:125`, Major (Security & Privacy). Deferred as a project-wide consistency change rather than a piecemeal Phase-61-only edit. The Phase 61 security review separately PASSed key handling (no caller-buffer mutation, no key logging).\n"
}
